[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-10-31
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Park#2814 at 18:00', 'Plaza#206 at 20:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "Pool#86 at 07:00 and Hotel#1377 at 08:00 suggests a morning routine, but the large gap until Park#2814 at 18:00 is unusual without further context."
}
```
==================== END RECORD ====================

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-02
- Trajectory: ['Buddhist Temple#854 at 00:10', 'Noodle House#690 at 00:30', 'Soba Restaurant#578 at 13:00', 'Arcade#751 at 18:00', 'Karaoke Bar#174 at 21:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`

Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-05
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Park#3527 at 18:00', 'Plaza#206 at 20:00', 'Event Space#104 at 22:00']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-07
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 07:30', 'Park#2814 at 19:00', 'Plaza#206 at 21:00', 'Convenience Store#9352 at 23:40']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
"The duration between 'Hotel#1377 at 07:30' and 'Park#2814 at 19:00' is 11.5 hours, which is an unusually long gap for a typical daily activity sequence without any specified intermediate events."
```

**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-10
- Trajectory: ['Park#3527 at 08:00', 'Farmers Market#81 at 10:00', 'Discount Store#300 at 11:00', 'Chinese Restaurant#14 at 12:30', 'Clothing Store#1075 at 14:00', 'Park#2814 at 15:30', 'Plaza#206 at 17:30', 'Soba Restaurant#578 at 19:00', 'Discount Store#10 at 20:30', 'Noodle House#690 at 21:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.

- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Objective]:
Critic_Check

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-11
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Farmers Market#81 at 18:00', 'Clothing Store#1075 at 19:00', 'Udon Restaurant#1855 at 20:00', 'Plaza#206 at 21:30', 'Event Space#104 at 22:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json

  "evaluation": "The gap between 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00' and 'Farmers Market#81 at 18:00' is 10 hours, which is a very long duration for an implied activity like travel or resting between these two events."
}

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-12
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 07:45', 'Plaza#206 at 19:30', 'Event Space#104 at 21:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:30']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "The time elapsed between 'Hotel#1377 at 07:45' and 'Plaza#206 at 19:30' is 11 hours and 45 minutes, which is an unusually long duration for implied travel or a single activity."
}
```

Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-12
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Park#2410 at 08:00', 'Plaza#206 at 19:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:50']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
{
  "evaluation": "Pass"
}
```
==================== END RECORD ====================

you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-13
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Park#2814 at 19:00', 'Plaza#206 at 21:00', 'Noodle House#690 at 23:00']

1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?

you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.

[Objective]:

[Prompt]:
Role: you are an expert urban data analyst and human behavior specialist.
Task: your goal is to critically evaluate a person's daily activity trajectory for semantic and logical plausibility based on common sense and typical urban life patterns.
you will be given the date and a sequence of activities. Each activity is a string with a location/action and a timestamp.
**Input Data:**
- Date: 2019-11-15
- Trajectory: ['Pool#86 at 07:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 08:00', 'Bank#66 at 17:00', 'Plaza#206 at 20:00', 'Hotel#1377 at 23:50']
**Critical Thinking Checklist:**
1.  **Temporal Logic (Time-based Sense):**
    -   **Opening Hours:** Is the venue (e.g., bank, store, educational institution) likely to be open at the specified time? Consider the day of the week (weekday/weekend/holiday) implied by the date.
    -   **Activity-Time Congruence:** Does the activity make sense at that time of day? (e.g., visiting a nightclub at 9 AM is unusual).
    -   **Duration Plausibility:** Is the time elapsed between consecutive events reasonable for the implied activity? For example, a 5-hour gap for "Travel to work" within the same city is highly suspicious. Pay close attention to long durations covered by `(Implied)` activities.
2.  **Sequential & Causal Logic (Order of Events):**
    -   **Common Sense Flow:** Does the sequence of activities follow a logical progression? (e.g., People usually go grocery shopping before returning home, not after).
    -   **Daily Rhythm:** Does the trajectory represent a coherent part of a day? Does it start and end in a plausible manner (e.g., most daily trajectories start or end at a residential location, even if not explicitly stated)?
**Output Format:**
you MUST respond with a JSON object. Do not add any text before or after the JSON.
- If the plan is reasonable, output exactly: "Pass"
- If there are logical errors, briefly explain the specific error in one sentence.
**Examples:**
-   **Example 1 (Plausible):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-05 (Monday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Home at 08:00", "Office#123 at 09:00", "Restaurant#45 at 12:15", "Office#123 at 13:30", "Home at 18:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Pass"`
-   **Example 2 (Temporal Flaw):**
    -   Date: 2026-01-06 (Tuesday)
    -   Trajectory: ["Restaurant#50 at 22:00", "Bank#789 at 23:00"]
    -   Expected Output: `"Banks are typically closed at 23:00 on a weekday."`
Now, analyze the provided input data and generate your response.

[Result]:
```json
"Banks are typically closed at 17:00 on a Friday, and the time elapsed between the bank and the plaza is also quite long."
```
